Like, an IV pump will beep—and honestly, the new pumps they just bought for our unit have this incredibly shrill tone. I don't know who in administration approved them, probably someone who never actually has to listen to them for twelve hours a day. My coworker Jenna was just complaining about it yesterday because she gets migraines, and I totally get why. \n\nBut anyway... where was I? Oh, right. A pump beeps, or someone asks another nurse a question, or a call light goes off, and suddenly I’ve completely forgotten what patient flow issue I was working on. It just vanishes from my head. And half the time the call light is just for, like, extra ice chips or a warm blanket, which is fine, I love helping my patients, but it completely derails me. It's just getting really bad.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing these memory lapses or getting distracted at home or in other areas of your life, outside of work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's definitely not just at work. It's everywhere. Like, I’ll completely forget things that should just be routine by now. I'll mean to call my mom back—she lives in Florida now, she just moved there last year actually, right near Sarasota. It's beautiful, but the humidity is just insane. I went to visit her in July and I swear I sweat through three shirts a day. I don't know how she stands it, but she says she prefers it to the winters up here. \n\nBut yeah, I'll mean to call her back, or I'll forget to pay a bill at home, or even miss an appointment. I've missed two dentist appointments this year. And the receptionist there is so passive-aggressive about it, like I'm doing it on purpose. I'm really not! Even when I set a reminder on my phone, I just... I don't know, I swipe it away or get distracted by an email and then it's gone. Same thing happens if I need to follow up on a missing order for a patient. It's just... poof. Gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or overwhelmed when you forget things or get distracted?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I guess I do get anxious sometimes, but honestly, I'm just more frustrated than anything. It's like, I know I'm smart, you know? I made it through nursing school, which was a nightmare. My clinical instructor, Mrs. Higgins, she was the absolute worst. She would literally stand over your shoulder while you were drawing up meds and just breathe down your neck. I swear she enjoyed making us nervous. One time she yelled at my friend Sarah so badly that Sarah actually threw up in the utility room. Sarah's a nurse practitioner now, actually, up in Boston. We keep saying we're going to get together, but it's just so hard to coordinate schedules. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I don't really know if \"anxious\" is the right word. I just feel like I'm constantly dropping the ball. Like I'm always one step behind where I'm supposed to be, and it's exhausting trying to catch up.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel sad or down when you're not able to keep up with things?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I feel sad, really. Just... tired. Like, I don't sit around crying about it. It's more of a heavy feeling, you know? Like when you work three twelves in a row and your feet just ache. Actually, I finally bought those compression socks everyone on my unit raves about. The ones with the little stethoscopes printed on them? They're super cute, but honestly, getting them on is like a full-body workout. My dog, Buster, he thought it was a game and kept trying to bite the toe while I was pulling them up. He's a golden retriever mix, the sweetest thing, but he has zero concept of personal space. He actually ate a whole stick of butter off the counter last week. I had to call poison control just to be safe, which felt ridiculous, but you never know. \n\nBut no, I'm not really sad or down. Just annoyed with myself, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having trouble focusing on tasks or activities, even when you're not being distracted by something else?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. It's like my brain just glazes over even if it's completely quiet and nothing is going on. Like, I’ll think I’ve perfectly finished an EHR audit or checked a transfer note, and then my supervisor will pull me aside. Her name is Brenda, she's actually retiring next month and they're throwing this huge potluck for her in the breakroom. I signed up to bring my baked mac and cheese, which is a recipe I got from my aunt. She puts a little bit of dry mustard and nutmeg in it, which sounds weird, but it totally works. It really cuts the richness of the sharp cheddar. \n\nBut anyway, Brenda will pull up my chart and find that I missed a required field somewhere, or I put the wrong time on a medication follow-up. Or I'll have copied the completely wrong room number, even though I swear I looked right at it when I was typing. It's so embarrassing. Speaking of room numbers, they just renumbered our entire West Wing because of the new cardiac unit they built, and it is a complete maze now. The poor pharmacy delivery guys just wander around the halls looking so confused. I had to rescue one yesterday who was trying to find the staff elevator.\n\nDoctor: Did these problems with distraction and memory start after something particularly stressful or upsetting happened in your life?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, there wasn't one big thing that happened. It's just always been like this, honestly. Like, even back in elementary school, I remember my teachers always writing on my report cards that I needed to \"apply myself\" more. My third-grade teacher, Mr. Davies, he had this giant fish tank in the back of the classroom. It was amazing. He had these neon tetras and a plecostomus that just stuck to the glass all day. I used to stare at that tank instead of doing my math worksheets. I actually tried to set up a small aquarium in my apartment a few years ago, thinking it would be relaxing, you know? But keeping the water pH balanced is surprisingly complicated. I ended up giving the whole setup to my neighbor's kid. He’s super into marine biology now, which is pretty cool. \n\nBut yeah, no specific stressful event or anything. It’s just... always been how my brain works, as far back as I can remember.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I don't feel like I have to get up and pace around or anything. I can sit through a whole movie just fine, usually. Unless it's one of those really long superhero movies. My brother dragged me to see the latest one, and I swear it was like three hours long. We got the giant tub of popcorn, the one where they give you free refills? But honestly, who gets up in the middle of a movie to get more popcorn? You'd miss the best parts. Plus, the floor in that theater was so sticky, it sounded like Velcro every time someone walked down the aisle. I kept thinking about how many germs must be on the bottom of my shoes. I actually keep a little bottle of hand sanitizer clipped to my badge reel at work, the kind that smells like cucumber melon. It reminds me of middle school, honestly, everybody used to wear that body spray. \n\nBut no, I don't really get restless or fidgety. I just sit there. Sometimes I'll pick at my cuticles, but that's just a bad habit.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I'm impulsive, no. I'm actually pretty careful about most things. Like at work, obviously, you have to be. I double-check all my IV lines, even if it takes me forever because I keep losing my place on the MAR. But in my personal life... I don't know, I guess I'm not the type to just jump into things without thinking. Well, except for maybe when I'm grocery shopping. I always go in with a list, but then I see something random, like this weird brand of pickled asparagus I found last week. I didn't even know I liked pickled asparagus, but the jar had this really pretty vintage label on it. I'm a sucker for good packaging. My whole bathroom is full of these expensive lotions I bought just because the bottles looked nice on the shelf. They're all eucalyptus and mint scented, which is supposed to be good for stress relief. I read this article once that said keeping eucalyptus in your shower can clear your sinuses, so I actually bought a whole bundle of it from the farmer's market. But it dried out really fast and just made a huge mess all over the tile. I had to scrub the grout with an old toothbrush to get all the little crumbly bits out. \n\nBut as far as doing big, impulsive things without thinking? No, not really. I don't just, like, quit my job or buy a car on a whim or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble following instructions or paying attention in quiet environments, like when you're reading a book or working on a puzzle?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. Quiet environments are actually the hardest for me. If I try to read a book, I might get through half a page before I realize I haven't absorbed a single word because I started thinking about something else. Like, the other day I tried to read this mystery novel my sister lent me. She’s obsessed with true crime podcasts, too, the ones where they solve cold cases? She listens to them while she's gardening. She has this amazing tomato garden in her backyard. Last summer she had so many tomatoes she started canning her own salsa. I tried to help her one weekend, but the boiling water bath process is so tedious. You have to sterilize all the jars and make sure the seals are perfect, otherwise you can give yourself botulism, which is terrifying. We learned about that in nursing school, actually. The neurotoxin just completely paralyzes you. It’s wild how something so tiny can do that much damage. But anyway, reading... yeah. Puzzles are the same. I just can't stay focused on them if it's quiet. I end up just staring into space.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with distraction and memory are affecting your self-esteem?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really is. It’s... it’s actually getting pretty bad. I feel like I'm constantly messing up, especially at work. It’s gotten to the point where I’m actually on formal supervision right now. The other nurses have to double-check my charge-nurse follow-through, which is just... it's humiliating, honestly. I'm supposed to be a leader on the floor, and instead, I feel like I’m becoming a liability. And the other nurses are so nice about it, which almost makes it worse? Like, Sarah, the other charge nurse, she always brings in these amazing baked goods for the breakroom. Last week she made these lemon blueberry scones that were incredible. She said the secret is freezing the butter before you grate it into the flour. I tried making scones once, but they turned out like hockey pucks. I think I overworked the dough. Baking is so precise, you know? It’s not like cooking where you can just throw in a little bit of this and a little bit of that. My grandmother used to make the best chicken soup without ever measuring anything. She’d just taste the broth and know exactly what it needed. She had this huge cast-iron pot that she used for everything. I think my mom still has it... \n\nBut yeah, the supervision at work is really taking a toll on me. I just feel like I can't trust my own brain anymore, and it's making me feel terrible about myself.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble completing everyday tasks at home due to your difficulties with distraction and memory?\n\nPatient: Yeah, home is... it's a mess, honestly. I forget things that should just be completely routine. Like paying a bill, or calling my mom back. She gets so frustrated with me. Or I'll completely miss an appointment, even when I meant to go and I swear I set a reminder on my phone. Sometimes I think my phone is glitching and deleting my reminders, but my husband says I just swipe them away without looking. He actually got me this physical planner, one of those really nice leather-bound ones? It's beautiful, the leather smells so good. It reminds me of this saddle shop near where I grew up. We used to go horseback riding every summer. My favorite horse was this sweet little chestnut mare named Buttercup. She was so gentle, but she had this funny habit of stopping to eat clover right in the middle of a trail ride. You'd have to pull her head up and click your tongue to get her moving again. I always brought her carrots, though. I read that horses love peppermints, too, but I was always too scared she'd accidentally bite my fingers. Their teeth are huge, you know? \n\nBut yeah, I forget to do basic things all the time at home. Like following up on an online order that never showed up. I just... it completely falls out of my head.\n\nDoctor: Are your difficulties with distraction and memory affecting your relationships with family and friends?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, honestly. I haven't noticed my friends being upset with me or anything. I mean, my husband gets frustrated sometimes, like I said, but my friends... we mostly just text or grab coffee. Actually, I met up with my friend Chloe for coffee last week at this new place that opened up near the hospital. They do all these fancy latte art things. She got a matcha latte and it had a perfect little swan drawn in the foam. I don't even know how they do that. I tried to froth milk at home once with one of those little hand-held wands, but it just splashed everywhere. It got all over my favorite sweater, this really soft cashmere one I found at a thrift store. \n\nThrift shopping is like a treasure hunt, you know? You have to be willing to dig through racks of junk, but sometimes you find something incredible with the tags still on. My sister found a vintage denim jacket once that fit her perfectly. She wore it to a concert we went to a few years ago... I think it was an outdoor music festival. It rained the whole time, and we were covered in mud, but it was so fun. The food trucks there were amazing, too. We had these Korean BBQ tacos that had this spicy slaw on top. I've tried to recreate that slaw at home, but I can never get the dressing quite right. It always ends up too sweet or too vinegary. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, relationships. Um, yeah, I don't think it's causing any major problems with my friends. At least, I haven't really noticed if it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant strain or impact on your relationships with your husband or others due to your difficulties with distraction and memory?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I don't really think it's causing a major strain. I mean, my husband definitely gets annoyed when I forget to do things around the house, like I mentioned before, but we don't really fight about it. It's more just him sighing loudly and doing it himself. He's actually really patient, usually. He works in IT, so I think he's just used to fixing things when they break or don't work right. He's always building these crazy computers with all the lights inside them. The fans are so loud, though. Sometimes I can hear them humming from the living room when I'm trying to watch TV. \n\nWe've been watching this new true-crime documentary series lately. It's fascinating, but it makes me so paranoid. I double-check the locks on the doors like three times before bed now. Our front door has this weird deadbolt that sticks if you don't pull the handle towards you while you turn the key. The landlord said he'd fix it months ago, but he never sent anyone out. He's a nice guy, but he's terrible at following up. He runs a hardware store downtown, one of those old-school ones where they still have wooden floors and sell individual screws out of little bins. I love places like that. They smell like sawdust and metal. My dad used to take me to one when I was a kid to get supplies for his woodworking projects. He made me this beautiful cedar chest for my dolls... \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I don't think my memory stuff is ruining my marriage or anything. It's just a little annoying for him sometimes, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had difficulty with distraction and memory, even as a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really has been a lifelong thing. Even back in elementary school, my teachers were always writing on my report cards that I was bright but careless. I was constantly losing my worksheets or just completely forgetting to turn in my homework, even if I had actually finished it. My mom had to sit with me every single night at the kitchen table just to keep me on track. \n\nIt was this huge, heavy oak table my parents got at an estate sale. It had these really cool carved legs, like lion paws holding glass balls. I used to just stare at them while I was supposed to be doing my spelling words. That table was right next to this big bay window, and my dad set up a bird feeder right outside of it. We'd get all kinds of birds... cardinals, blue jays, and sometimes these tiny little yellow finches. The squirrels would always try to steal the seeds, though. We had this one squirrel that was missing half its tail, and he was so bold he would climb right up the screen door. My dad tried everything to keep him away. He even bought this spicy birdseed that’s supposed to burn the squirrels' mouths so they leave it alone, but I swear that squirrel actually liked it. He just kept coming back. We ended up naming him Jalapeño. \n\nBut um... what was the question again? Oh, right, being a kid. Yeah, my mom had to literally sit there and watch me do my homework every night because I'd just lose everything otherwise.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I don't really know, to be honest. I mean, I guess it feels like both? Sometimes I'm definitely bored, especially during those long shift huddles at the hospital. They just drag on and on. It's just getting really bad.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing these memory lapses or getting distracted at home or in other areas of your life, outside of work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's definitely not just at work. It's everywhere. Like, I’ll completely forget things that should just be routine by now. I'll mean to call my mom back—she lives in Florida now, she just moved there last year actually, right near Sarasota. It's beautiful, but the humidity is just insane. Same thing happens if I need to follow up on a missing order for a patient. It's just... poof. Gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or overwhelmed when you forget things or get distracted?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I guess I do get anxious sometimes, but honestly, I'm just more frustrated than anything. It's like, I know I'm smart, you know? I made it through nursing school, which was a nightmare. My clinical instructor, Mrs. Higgins, she was the absolute worst. She would literally stand over your shoulder while you were drawing up meds and just breathe down your neck. I swear she enjoyed making us nervous. One time she yelled at my friend Sarah so badly that Sarah actually threw up in the utility room. Sarah's a nurse practitioner now, actually, up in Boston. We keep saying we're going to get together, but it's just so hard to coordinate schedules. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I don't really know if \"anxious\" is the right word. I just feel like I'm constantly dropping the ball. Like I'm always one step behind where I'm supposed to be, and it's exhausting trying to catch up.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel sad or down when you're not able to keep up with things?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I feel sad, really. Just... tired. Like, I don't sit around crying about it. It's more of a heavy feeling, you know? Like when you work three twelves in a row and your feet just ache. Actually, I finally bought those compression socks everyone on my unit raves about. The ones with the little stethoscopes printed on them? They're super cute, but honestly, getting them on is like a full-body workout. My dog, Buster, he thought it was a game and kept trying to bite the toe while I was pulling them up. He's a golden retriever mix, the sweetest thing, but he has zero concept of personal space. He actually ate a whole stick of butter off the counter last week. I had to call poison control just to be safe, which felt ridiculous, but you never know. \n\nBut no, I'm not really sad or down. Just annoyed with myself, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having trouble focusing on tasks or activities, even when you're not being distracted by something else?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. It's like my brain just glazes over even if it's completely quiet and nothing is going on. Like, I’ll think I’ve perfectly finished an EHR audit or checked a transfer note, and then my supervisor will pull me aside. Her name is Brenda, she's actually retiring next month and they're throwing this huge potluck for her in the breakroom. I signed up to bring my baked mac and cheese, which is a recipe I got from my aunt. She puts a little bit of dry mustard and nutmeg in it, which sounds weird, but it totally works. It really cuts the richness of the sharp cheddar. \n\nBut anyway, Brenda will pull up my chart and find that I missed a required field somewhere, or I put the wrong time on a medication follow-up. Or I'll have copied the completely wrong room number, even though I swear I looked right at it when I was typing. It's so embarrassing. Speaking of room numbers, they just renumbered our entire West Wing because of the new cardiac unit they built, and it is a complete maze now. The poor pharmacy delivery guys just wander around the halls looking so confused. I had to rescue one yesterday who was trying to find the staff elevator.\n\nDoctor: Did these problems with distraction and memory start after something particularly stressful or upsetting happened in your life?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, there wasn't one big thing that happened. It's just always been like this, honestly. Like, even back in elementary school, I remember my teachers always writing on my report cards that I needed to \"apply myself\" more. My third-grade teacher, Mr. Davies, he had this giant fish tank in the back of the classroom. It was amazing. He had these neon tetras and a plecostomus that just stuck to the glass all day. I used to stare at that tank instead of doing my math worksheets. I actually tried to set up a small aquarium in my apartment a few years ago, thinking it would be relaxing, you know? But keeping the water pH balanced is surprisingly complicated. I ended up giving the whole setup to my neighbor's kid. He’s super into marine biology now, which is pretty cool. \n\nBut yeah, no specific stressful event or anything. It’s just... always been how my brain works, as far back as I can remember.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I don't feel like I have to get up and pace around or anything. I can sit through a whole movie just fine, usually. Unless it's one of those really long superhero movies. My brother dragged me to see the latest one, and I swear it was like three hours long. We got the giant tub of popcorn, the one where they give you free refills? But honestly, who gets up in the middle of a movie to get more popcorn? You'd miss the best parts. Plus, the floor in that theater was so sticky, it sounded like Velcro every time someone walked down the aisle. I kept thinking about how many germs must be on the bottom of my shoes. I actually keep a little bottle of hand sanitizer clipped to my badge reel at work, the kind that smells like cucumber melon. It reminds me of middle school, honestly, everybody used to wear that body spray. \n\nBut no, I don't really get restless or fidgety. I just sit there. Sometimes I'll pick at my cuticles, but that's just a bad habit.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I'm impulsive, no. I'm actually pretty careful about most things. Like at work, obviously, you have to be. I double-check all my IV lines, even if it takes me forever because I keep losing my place on the MAR. But in my personal life... I don't know, I guess I'm not the type to just jump into things without thinking. Well, except for maybe when I'm grocery shopping. I always go in with a list, but then I see something random, like this weird brand of pickled asparagus I found last week. I didn't even know I liked pickled asparagus, but the jar had this really pretty vintage label on it. I'm a sucker for good packaging. My whole bathroom is full of these expensive lotions I bought just because the bottles looked nice on the shelf. They're all eucalyptus and mint scented, which is supposed to be good for stress relief. I read this article once that said keeping eucalyptus in your shower can clear your sinuses, so I actually bought a whole bundle of it from the farmer's market. But it dried out really fast and just made a huge mess all over the tile. I had to scrub the grout with an old toothbrush to get all the little crumbly bits out. \n\nBut as far as doing big, impulsive things without thinking? No, not really. I don't just, like, quit my job or buy a car on a whim or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble following instructions or paying attention in quiet environments, like when you're reading a book or working on a puzzle?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. Quiet environments are actually the hardest for me. If I try to read a book, I might get through half a page before I realize I haven't absorbed a single word because I started thinking about something else. Like, the other day I tried to read this mystery novel my sister lent me. She’s obsessed with true crime podcasts, too, the ones where they solve cold cases? She listens to them while she's gardening. She has this amazing tomato garden in her backyard. Last summer she had so many tomatoes she started canning her own salsa. I tried to help her one weekend, but the boiling water bath process is so tedious. You have to sterilize all the jars and make sure the seals are perfect, otherwise you can give yourself botulism, which is terrifying. We learned about that in nursing school, actually. The neurotoxin just completely paralyzes you. It’s wild how something so tiny can do that much damage. But anyway, reading... yeah. Puzzles are the same. I just can't stay focused on them if it's quiet. I end up just staring into space.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with distraction and memory are affecting your self-esteem?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really is. It’s... it’s actually getting pretty bad. I feel like I'm constantly messing up, especially at work. It’s gotten to the point where I’m actually on formal supervision right now. The other nurses have to double-check my charge-nurse follow-through, which is just... it's humiliating, honestly. I'm supposed to be a leader on the floor, and instead, I feel like I’m becoming a liability. And the other nurses are so nice about it, which almost makes it worse? Like, Sarah, the other charge nurse, she always brings in these amazing baked goods for the breakroom. Last week she made these lemon blueberry scones that were incredible. She said the secret is freezing the butter before you grate it into the flour. I tried making scones once, but they turned out like hockey pucks. I think I overworked the dough. Baking is so precise, you know? It’s not like cooking where you can just throw in a little bit of this and a little bit of that. My grandmother used to make the best chicken soup without ever measuring anything. She’d just taste the broth and know exactly what it needed. She had this huge cast-iron pot that she used for everything. I think my mom still has it... \n\nBut yeah, the supervision at work is really taking a toll on me. I just feel like I can't trust my own brain anymore, and it's making me feel terrible about myself.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble completing everyday tasks at home due to your difficulties with distraction and memory?\n\nPatient: Yeah, home is... it's a mess, honestly. I forget things that should just be completely routine. Like paying a bill, or calling my mom back. She gets so frustrated with me. Or I'll completely miss an appointment, even when I meant to go and I swear I set a reminder on my phone. Sometimes I think my phone is glitching and deleting my reminders, but my husband says I just swipe them away without looking. He actually got me this physical planner, one of those really nice leather-bound ones? It's beautiful, the leather smells so good. It reminds me of this saddle shop near where I grew up. We used to go horseback riding every summer. My favorite horse was this sweet little chestnut mare named Buttercup. She was so gentle, but she had this funny habit of stopping to eat clover right in the middle of a trail ride. You'd have to pull her head up and click your tongue to get her moving again. I always brought her carrots, though. I read that horses love peppermints, too, but I was always too scared she'd accidentally bite my fingers. Their teeth are huge, you know? \n\nBut yeah, I forget to do basic things all the time at home. Like following up on an online order that never showed up. I just... it completely falls out of my head.\n\nDoctor: Are your difficulties with distraction and memory affecting your relationships with family and friends?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, honestly. I haven't noticed my friends being upset with me or anything. I mean, my husband gets frustrated sometimes, like I said, but my friends... we mostly just text or grab coffee. Actually, I met up with my friend Chloe for coffee last week at this new place that opened up near the hospital. They do all these fancy latte art things. She got a matcha latte and it had a perfect little swan drawn in the foam. I don't even know how they do that. I tried to froth milk at home once with one of those little hand-held wands, but it just splashed everywhere. It got all over my favorite sweater, this really soft cashmere one I found at a thrift store. \n\nThrift shopping is like a treasure hunt, you know? You have to be willing to dig through racks of junk, but sometimes you find something incredible with the tags still on. My sister found a vintage denim jacket once that fit her perfectly. She wore it to a concert we went to a few years ago... I think it was an outdoor music festival. It rained the whole time, and we were covered in mud, but it was so fun. The food trucks there were amazing, too. We had these Korean BBQ tacos that had this spicy slaw on top. I've tried to recreate that slaw at home, but I can never get the dressing quite right. It always ends up too sweet or too vinegary. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, relationships. Um, yeah, I don't think it's causing any major problems with my friends. At least, I haven't really noticed if it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant strain or impact on your relationships with your husband or others due to your difficulties with distraction and memory?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I don't really think it's causing a major strain. I mean, my husband definitely gets annoyed when I forget to do things around the house, like I mentioned before, but we don't really fight about it. It's more just him sighing loudly and doing it himself. He's actually really patient, usually. He works in IT, so I think he's just used to fixing things when they break or don't work right. He's always building these crazy computers with all the lights inside them. The fans are so loud, though. Sometimes I can hear them humming from the living room when I'm trying to watch TV. \n\nWe've been watching this new true-crime documentary series lately. It's fascinating, but it makes me so paranoid. I double-check the locks on the doors like three times before bed now. Our front door has this weird deadbolt that sticks if you don't pull the handle towards you while you turn the key. The landlord said he'd fix it months ago, but he never sent anyone out. He's a nice guy, but he's terrible at following up. He runs a hardware store downtown, one of those old-school ones where they still have wooden floors and sell individual screws out of little bins. I love places like that. They smell like sawdust and metal. My dad used to take me to one when I was a kid to get supplies for his woodworking projects. He made me this beautiful cedar chest for my dolls... \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I don't think my memory stuff is ruining my marriage or anything. It's just a little annoying for him sometimes, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had difficulty with distraction and memory, even as a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really has been a lifelong thing. Even back in elementary school, my teachers were always writing on my report cards that I was bright but careless. I was constantly losing my worksheets or just completely forgetting to turn in my homework, even if I had actually finished it. My mom had to sit with me every single night at the kitchen table just to keep me on track. \n\nIt was this huge, heavy oak table my parents got at an estate sale. It had these really cool carved legs, like lion paws holding glass balls. I used to just stare at them while I was supposed to be doing my spelling words. That table was right next to this big bay window, and my dad set up a bird feeder right outside of it. We'd get all kinds of birds... cardinals, blue jays, and sometimes these tiny little yellow finches. The squirrels would always try to steal the seeds, though. We had this one squirrel that was missing half its tail, and he was so bold he would climb right up the screen door. My dad tried everything to keep him away. He even bought this spicy birdseed that’s supposed to burn the squirrels' mouths so they leave it alone, but I swear that squirrel actually liked it. He just kept coming back. We ended up naming him Jalapeño. \n\nBut um... what was the question again? Oh, right, being a kid. Yeah, my mom had to literally sit there and watch me do my homework every night because I'd just lose everything otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind wanders because you're bored or because you're actually having trouble paying attention?\n\nPatient: I don't really know, to be honest. I mean, I guess it feels like both? Sometimes I'm definitely bored, especially during those long shift huddles at the hospital. They just drag on and on. Our charge nurse, Brenda, she means well, but she talks so slowly. She always wears these really bright, patterned scrubs—like, neon flamingos or huge sunflowers. I get that she’s trying to be cheerful for the patients, but it’s a lot to look at first thing in the morning. I bought a pair of holiday scrubs once, with little reindeer on them, but the material was so stiff and scratchy. I had to wash them like ten times with fabric softener before I could even wear them. I usually just stick to the solid navy ones now. They're way more comfortable, and you don't have to worry about matching them with anything. Plus, they hide coffee stains really well. I spilled a whole iced macchiato on myself in the breakroom last month. It was a disaster. The ice went everywhere, and one of the pieces slid right under the fridge. I had to get a broom handle to fish it out so it wouldn't melt and ruin the floor. \n\nWait, sorry, what did you ask me? Oh, right, my mind wandering. Yeah, I don't know if it's boredom or just not being able to focus. It just happens, no matter what I'm doing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. No, I don't think so. I'm not really a fidgety person. I mean, I sit still just fine. My husband is the one who can't sit still. She said the secret is freezing the butter before you grate it into the flour. I tried making scones once, but they turned out like hockey pucks. I think I overworked the dough. Baking is so precise, you know? It’s not like cooking where you can just throw in a little bit of this and a little bit of that. My grandmother used to make the best chicken soup without ever measuring anything. She’d just taste the broth and know exactly what it needed. She had this huge cast-iron pot that she used for everything. I think my mom still has it... \n\nBut yeah, the supervision at work is really taking a toll on me. I just feel like I can't trust my own brain anymore, and it's making me feel terrible about myself.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble completing everyday tasks at home due to your difficulties with distraction and memory?\n\nPatient: Yeah, home is... it's a mess, honestly. I forget things that should just be completely routine. Like paying a bill, or calling my mom back. She gets so frustrated with me. Or I'll completely miss an appointment, even when I meant to go and I swear I set a reminder on my phone. Sometimes I think my phone is glitching and deleting my reminders, but my husband says I just swipe them away without looking. He actually got me this physical planner, one of those really nice leather-bound ones? It's beautiful, the leather smells so good. It reminds me of this saddle shop near where I grew up. We used to go horseback riding every summer. My favorite horse was this sweet little chestnut mare named Buttercup. She was so gentle, but she had this funny habit of stopping to eat clover right in the middle of a trail ride. You'd have to pull her head up and click your tongue to get her moving again. I always brought her carrots, though. I read that horses love peppermints, too, but I was always too scared she'd accidentally bite my fingers. Their teeth are huge, you know? \n\nBut yeah, I forget to do basic things all the time at home. Like following up on an online order that never showed up. I just... it completely falls out of my head.\n\nDoctor: Are your difficulties with distraction and memory affecting your relationships with family and friends?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, honestly. I haven't noticed my friends being upset with me or anything. I mean, my husband gets frustrated sometimes, like I said, but my friends... we mostly just text or grab coffee. Actually, I met up with my friend Chloe for coffee last week at this new place that opened up near the hospital. They do all these fancy latte art things. She got a matcha latte and it had a perfect little swan drawn in the foam. I don't even know how they do that. I tried to froth milk at home once with one of those little hand-held wands, but it just splashed everywhere. It got all over my favorite sweater, this really soft cashmere one I found at a thrift store. \n\nThrift shopping is like a treasure hunt, you know? You have to be willing to dig through racks of junk, but sometimes you find something incredible with the tags still on. My sister found a vintage denim jacket once that fit her perfectly. She wore it to a concert we went to a few years ago... I think it was an outdoor music festival. It rained the whole time, and we were covered in mud, but it was so fun. The food trucks there were amazing, too. We had these Korean BBQ tacos that had this spicy slaw on top. I've tried to recreate that slaw at home, but I can never get the dressing quite right. It always ends up too sweet or too vinegary. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, relationships. Um, yeah, I don't think it's causing any major problems with my friends. At least, I haven't really noticed if it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant strain or impact on your relationships with your husband or others due to your difficulties with distraction and memory?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I don't really think it's causing a major strain. I mean, my husband definitely gets annoyed when I forget to do things around the house, like I mentioned before, but we don't really fight about it. It's more just him sighing loudly and doing it himself. He's actually really patient, usually. He works in IT, so I think he's just used to fixing things when they break or don't work right. He's always building these crazy computers with all the lights inside them. The fans are so loud, though. Sometimes I can hear them humming from the living room when I'm trying to watch TV. \n\nWe've been watching this new true-crime documentary series lately. It's fascinating, but it makes me so paranoid. I double-check the locks on the doors like three times before bed now. Our front door has this weird deadbolt that sticks if you don't pull the handle towards you while you turn the key. The landlord said he'd fix it months ago, but he never sent anyone out. He's a nice guy, but he's terrible at following up. He runs a hardware store downtown, one of those old-school ones where they still have wooden floors and sell individual screws out of little bins. I love places like that. They smell like sawdust and metal. My dad used to take me to one when I was a kid to get supplies for his woodworking projects. He made me this beautiful cedar chest for my dolls... \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I don't think my memory stuff is ruining my marriage or anything. It's just a little annoying for him sometimes, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had difficulty with distraction and memory, even as a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really has been a lifelong thing. Even back in elementary school, my teachers were always writing on my report cards that I was bright but careless. I was constantly losing my worksheets or just completely forgetting to turn in my homework, even if I had actually finished it. My mom had to sit with me every single night at the kitchen table just to keep me on track. \n\nIt was this huge, heavy oak table my parents got at an estate sale. It had these really cool carved legs, like lion paws holding glass balls. I used to just stare at them while I was supposed to be doing my spelling words. That table was right next to this big bay window, and my dad set up a bird feeder right outside of it. We'd get all kinds of birds... cardinals, blue jays, and sometimes these tiny little yellow finches. The squirrels would always try to steal the seeds, though. We had this one squirrel that was missing half its tail, and he was so bold he would climb right up the screen door. My dad tried everything to keep him away. He even bought this spicy birdseed that’s supposed to burn the squirrels' mouths so they leave it alone, but I swear that squirrel actually liked it. He just kept coming back. We ended up naming him Jalapeño. \n\nBut um... what was the question again? Oh, right, being a kid. Yeah, my mom had to literally sit there and watch me do my homework every night because I'd just lose everything otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind wanders because you're bored or because you're actually having trouble paying attention?\n\nPatient: I don't really know, to be honest. I mean, I guess it feels like both? Sometimes I'm definitely bored, especially during those long shift huddles at the hospital. They just drag on and on. Our charge nurse, Brenda, she means well, but she talks so slowly. She always wears these really bright, patterned scrubs—like, neon flamingos or huge sunflowers. I get that she’s trying to be cheerful for the patients, but it’s a lot to look at first thing in the morning. I bought a pair of holiday scrubs once, with little reindeer on them, but the material was so stiff and scratchy. I had to wash them like ten times with fabric softener before I could even wear them. I usually just stick to the solid navy ones now. They're way more comfortable, and you don't have to worry about matching them with anything. Plus, they hide coffee stains really well. I spilled a whole iced macchiato on myself in the breakroom last month. It was a disaster. The ice went everywhere, and one of the pieces slid right under the fridge. I had to get a broom handle to fish it out so it wouldn't melt and ruin the floor. \n\nWait, sorry, what did you ask me? Oh, right, my mind wandering. Yeah, I don't know if it's boredom or just not being able to focus. It just happens, no matter what I'm doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're moving or fidgeting when you're supposed to be still?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I'm not really a fidgety person. I mean, I sit still just fine. My husband is the one who can't sit still. He's always tapping his foot or bouncing his leg when we're watching TV. It drives me crazy sometimes, especially when we're watching something tense, like that true-crime show I mentioned. He does this thing where he clicks his pen, too, when he's working at his desk. Click, click, click. I bought him this really nice fountain pen for our anniversary last year, thinking maybe he'd stop clicking if he had a pen with a cap, you know? It was this deep burgundy color with gold trim. Really elegant. I got it engraved with his initials, too. But he says he's afraid to use it because he doesn't want to ruin the nib. He says fountain pens require a certain angle and pressure, and he's just used to ballpoints. So it just sits in its velvet box on his desk, and he keeps using those cheap plastic ones from the bank. \n\nBut yeah, me? No, I don't really fidget or feel like I have to move around. I'm pretty calm physically.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. No, I've never been diagnosed with it, and honestly, I never really considered it until recently. I always just thought ADHD was for little boys who bounce off the walls and can't sit in their chairs, you know? Like my nephew, Leo. He's seven, and he is just a tornado of energy. He's a golden retriever mix, the sweetest thing, but he has zero concept of personal space. He actually ate a whole stick of butter off the counter last week. I had to call poison control just to be safe, which felt ridiculous, but you never know. \n\nBut no, I'm not really sad or down. Just annoyed with myself, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having trouble focusing on tasks or activities, even when you're not being distracted by something else?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. It's like my brain just glazes over even if it's completely quiet and nothing is going on. Like, I’ll think I’ve perfectly finished an EHR audit or checked a transfer note, and then my supervisor will pull me aside. Her name is Brenda, she's actually retiring next month and they're throwing this huge potluck for her in the breakroom. I signed up to bring my baked mac and cheese, which is a recipe I got from my aunt. She puts a little bit of dry mustard and nutmeg in it, which sounds weird, but it totally works. It really cuts the richness of the sharp cheddar. \n\nBut anyway, Brenda will pull up my chart and find that I missed a required field somewhere, or I put the wrong time on a medication follow-up. Speaking of room numbers, they just renumbered our entire West Wing because of the new cardiac unit they built, and it is a complete maze now. The poor pharmacy delivery guys just wander around the halls looking so confused. I had to rescue one yesterday who was trying to find the staff elevator.\n\nDoctor: Did these problems with distraction and memory start after something particularly stressful or upsetting happened in your life?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, there wasn't one big thing that happened. It's just always been like this, honestly. Like, even back in elementary school, I remember my teachers always writing on my report cards that I needed to \"apply myself\" more. My third-grade teacher, Mr. Davies, he had this giant fish tank in the back of the classroom. It was amazing. He had these neon tetras and a plecostomus that just stuck to the glass all day. I used to stare at that tank instead of doing my math worksheets. I actually tried to set up a small aquarium in my apartment a few years ago, thinking it would be relaxing, you know? But keeping the water pH balanced is surprisingly complicated. I ended up giving the whole setup to my neighbor's kid. He’s super into marine biology now, which is pretty cool. \n\nBut yeah, no specific stressful event or anything. It’s just... always been how my brain works, as far back as I can remember.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I don't feel like I have to get up and pace around or anything. I can sit through a whole movie just fine, usually. Unless it's one of those really long superhero movies. But honestly, who gets up in the middle of a movie to get more popcorn? You'd miss the best parts. Sometimes I'll pick at my cuticles, but that's just a bad habit.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I'm impulsive, no. I'm actually pretty careful about most things. Like at work, obviously, you have to be. I double-check all my IV lines, even if it takes me forever because I keep losing my place on the MAR. But in my personal life... I don't know, I guess I'm not the type to just jump into things without thinking. Well, except for maybe when I'm grocery shopping. I always go in with a list, but then I see something random, like this weird brand of pickled asparagus I found last week. I didn't even know I liked pickled asparagus, but the jar had this really pretty vintage label on it. I'm a sucker for good packaging. My whole bathroom is full of these expensive lotions I bought just because the bottles looked nice on the shelf. They're all eucalyptus and mint scented, which is supposed to be good for stress relief. I read this article once that said keeping eucalyptus in your shower can clear your sinuses, so I actually bought a whole bundle of it from the farmer's market. But it dried out really fast and just made a huge mess all over the tile. I had to scrub the grout with an old toothbrush to get all the little crumbly bits out. \n\nBut as far as doing big, impulsive things without thinking? No, not really. I don't just, like, quit my job or buy a car on a whim or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble following instructions or paying attention in quiet environments, like when you're reading a book or working on a puzzle?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. Quiet environments are actually the hardest for me. If I try to read a book, I might get through half a page before I realize I haven't absorbed a single word because I started thinking about something else. Like, the other day I tried to read this mystery novel my sister lent me. She’s obsessed with true crime podcasts, too, the ones where they solve cold cases? She listens to them while she's gardening. She has this amazing tomato garden in her backyard. Last summer she had so many tomatoes she started canning her own salsa. I tried to help her one weekend, but the boiling water bath process is so tedious. You have to sterilize all the jars and make sure the seals are perfect, otherwise you can give yourself botulism, which is terrifying. We learned about that in nursing school, actually. The neurotoxin just completely paralyzes you. It’s wild how something so tiny can do that much damage. But anyway, reading... yeah. Puzzles are the same. I just can't stay focused on them if it's quiet. I end up just staring into space.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with distraction and memory are affecting your self-esteem?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really is. It’s... it’s actually getting pretty bad. I feel like I'm constantly messing up, especially at work. It’s gotten to the point where I’m actually on formal supervision right now. The other nurses have to double-check my charge-nurse follow-through, which is just... it's humiliating, honestly. I'm supposed to be a leader on the floor, and instead, I feel like I’m becoming a liability. And the other nurses are so nice about it, which almost makes it worse? Like, Sarah, the other charge nurse, she always brings in these amazing baked goods for the breakroom. Last week she made these lemon blueberry scones that were incredible. She said the secret is freezing the butter before you grate it into the flour. I tried making scones once, but they turned out like hockey pucks. I think I overworked the dough. Baking is so precise, you know? It’s not like cooking where you can just throw in a little bit of this and a little bit of that. My grandmother used to make the best chicken soup without ever measuring anything. She’d just taste the broth and know exactly what it needed. She had this huge cast-iron pot that she used for everything. I think my mom still has it... \n\nBut yeah, the supervision at work is really taking a toll on me. I just feel like I can't trust my own brain anymore, and it's making me feel terrible about myself.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble completing everyday tasks at home due to your difficulties with distraction and memory?\n\nPatient: Yeah, home is... it's a mess, honestly. I forget things that should just be completely routine. Like paying a bill, or calling my mom back. She gets so frustrated with me. Sometimes I think my phone is glitching and deleting my reminders, but my husband says I just swipe them away without looking. He actually got me this physical planner, one of those really nice leather-bound ones? It's beautiful, the leather smells so good. It reminds me of this saddle shop near where I grew up. We used to go horseback riding every summer. My favorite horse was this sweet little chestnut mare named Buttercup. She was so gentle, but she had this funny habit of stopping to eat clover right in the middle of a trail ride. You'd have to pull her head up and click your tongue to get her moving again. I always brought her carrots, though. I read that horses love peppermints, too, but I was always too scared she'd accidentally bite my fingers. Their teeth are huge, you know? \n\nBut yeah, I forget to do basic things all the time at home. Like following up on an online order that never showed up. I just... it completely falls out of my head.\n\nDoctor: Are your difficulties with distraction and memory affecting your relationships with family and friends?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, honestly. I haven't noticed my friends being upset with me or anything. I mean, my husband gets frustrated sometimes, like I said, but my friends... we mostly just text or grab coffee. Actually, I met up with my friend Chloe for coffee last week at this new place that opened up near the hospital. They do all these fancy latte art things. She got a matcha latte and it had a perfect little swan drawn in the foam. I don't even know how they do that. I tried to froth milk at home once with one of those little hand-held wands, but it just splashed everywhere. It got all over my favorite sweater, this really soft cashmere one I found at a thrift store. \n\nThrift shopping is like a treasure hunt, you know? You have to be willing to dig through racks of junk, but sometimes you find something incredible with the tags still on. My sister found a vintage denim jacket once that fit her perfectly. She wore it to a concert we went to a few years ago... I think it was an outdoor music festival. It rained the whole time, and we were covered in mud, but it was so fun. The food trucks there were amazing, too. We had these Korean BBQ tacos that had this spicy slaw on top. I've tried to recreate that slaw at home, but I can never get the dressing quite right. It always ends up too sweet or too vinegary. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, relationships. Um, yeah, I don't think it's causing any major problems with my friends. At least, I haven't really noticed if it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant strain or impact on your relationships with your husband or others due to your difficulties with distraction and memory?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I don't really think it's causing a major strain. I mean, my husband definitely gets annoyed when I forget to do things around the house, like I mentioned before, but we don't really fight about it. It's more just him sighing loudly and doing it himself. He's actually really patient, usually. He works in IT, so I think he's just used to fixing things when they break or don't work right. He's always building these crazy computers with all the lights inside them. The fans are so loud, though. Sometimes I can hear them humming from the living room when I'm trying to watch TV. \n\nWe've been watching this new true-crime documentary series lately. It's fascinating, but it makes me so paranoid. I double-check the locks on the doors like three times before bed now. Our front door has this weird deadbolt that sticks if you don't pull the handle towards you while you turn the key. The landlord said he'd fix it months ago, but he never sent anyone out. He's a nice guy, but he's terrible at following up. He runs a hardware store downtown, one of those old-school ones where they still have wooden floors and sell individual screws out of little bins. I love places like that. They smell like sawdust and metal. My dad used to take me to one when I was a kid to get supplies for his woodworking projects. He made me this beautiful cedar chest for my dolls... \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I don't think my memory stuff is ruining my marriage or anything. It's just a little annoying for him sometimes, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had difficulty with distraction and memory, even as a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really has been a lifelong thing. Even back in elementary school, my teachers were always writing on my report cards that I was bright but careless. I was constantly losing my worksheets or just completely forgetting to turn in my homework, even if I had actually finished it. My mom had to sit with me every single night at the kitchen table just to keep me on track. \n\nIt was this huge, heavy oak table my parents got at an estate sale. It had these really cool carved legs, like lion paws holding glass balls. I used to just stare at them while I was supposed to be doing my spelling words. That table was right next to this big bay window, and my dad set up a bird feeder right outside of it. We'd get all kinds of birds... cardinals, blue jays, and sometimes these tiny little yellow finches. The squirrels would always try to steal the seeds, though. We had this one squirrel that was missing half its tail, and he was so bold he would climb right up the screen door. My dad tried everything to keep him away. He even bought this spicy birdseed that’s supposed to burn the squirrels' mouths so they leave it alone, but I swear that squirrel actually liked it. He just kept coming back. We ended up naming him Jalapeño. \n\nBut um... what was the question again? Oh, right, being a kid. Yeah, my mom had to literally sit there and watch me do my homework every night because I'd just lose everything otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind wanders because you're bored or because you're actually having trouble paying attention?\n\nPatient: I don't really know, to be honest. I mean, I guess it feels like both? Sometimes I'm definitely bored, especially during those long shift huddles at the hospital. They just drag on and on. Our charge nurse, Brenda, she means well, but she talks so slowly. She always wears these really bright, patterned scrubs—like, neon flamingos or huge sunflowers. I get that she’s trying to be cheerful for the patients, but it’s a lot to look at first thing in the morning. I bought a pair of holiday scrubs once, with little reindeer on them, but the material was so stiff and scratchy. I had to wash them like ten times with fabric softener before I could even wear them. I usually just stick to the solid navy ones now. They're way more comfortable, and you don't have to worry about matching them with anything. Plus, they hide coffee stains really well. I spilled a whole iced macchiato on myself in the breakroom last month. It was a disaster. The ice went everywhere, and one of the pieces slid right under the fridge. I had to get a broom handle to fish it out so it wouldn't melt and ruin the floor. \n\nWait, sorry, what did you ask me? Oh, right, my mind wandering. Yeah, I don't know if it's boredom or just not being able to focus. It just happens, no matter what I'm doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're moving or fidgeting when you're supposed to be still?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I'm not really a fidgety person. I mean, I sit still just fine. My husband is the one who can't sit still. He's always tapping his foot or bouncing his leg when we're watching TV. It drives me crazy sometimes, especially when we're watching something tense, like that true-crime show I mentioned. He does this thing where he clicks his pen, too, when he's working at his desk. Click, click, click. I bought him this really nice fountain pen for our anniversary last year, thinking maybe he'd stop clicking if he had a pen with a cap, you know? It was this deep burgundy color with gold trim. Really elegant. I got it engraved with his initials, too. But he says he's afraid to use it because he doesn't want to ruin the nib. He says fountain pens require a certain angle and pressure, and he's just used to ballpoints. So it just sits in its velvet box on his desk, and he keeps using those cheap plastic ones from the bank. \n\nBut yeah, me? No, I don't really fidget or feel like I have to move around. I'm pretty calm physically.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been diagnosed with or considered having ADHD?\n\nPatient: No, I've never been diagnosed with it, and honestly, I never really considered it until recently. I always just thought ADHD was for little boys who bounce off the walls and can't sit in their chairs, you know? Like my nephew, Leo. He's seven, and he is just a tornado of energy. My sister put him in martial arts hoping it would burn off some of that energy, but now he just karate chops everything in the house. He actually broke one of her favorite vases last Thanksgiving. It was this beautiful blue and white porcelain one she got on her honeymoon in Greece. They went to Santorini, and the pictures were just stunning. All those white buildings with the bright blue roofs against the ocean. I've always wanted to go to Greece, but getting the time off work is so hard. The hospital is always short-staffed, especially during the holidays. Last Christmas, I worked a twelve-hour shift and didn't even get to sit down for lunch. I just ate a granola bar while charting. It was one of those oats and honey ones, and the crumbs got all over my keyboard. I had to use a sticky note to try and get them out from between the keys. \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, ADHD. Yeah, no, I never got diagnosed or anything. I just thought I was scatterbrained or bad at organizing things.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I don't think so? I mean, I don't really get hyper-focused on anything, at least not that I've noticed. If anything, I'm usually juggling too many things at once and not really focusing on any of them. Like yesterday, I was trying to meal prep for the week, do laundry, and pay some bills online all at the same time. I ended up burning the quinoa because I got distracted trying to figure out why our water bill was so high. It was like twenty dollars more than last month, and we haven't even been watering the lawn because it's been raining so much. My husband thinks there might be a leak in the sprinkler system, but getting someone out to look at it is such a hassle. Last time we had a plumber over, he tracked mud all over the entryway rug. It's this cream-colored rug I found at a flea market, and it took me hours to scrub the stains out with this special enzyme cleaner I ordered online. It smelled like citrus, which was nice, but the scrubbing made my wrists ache for two days. I had to wear my compression braces to work, and trying to start an IV with those on is basically impossible. 

But yeah, no, I don't really get super focused on one thing. I'm usually just bouncing around trying to keep my head above water.

I had to call poison control just to be safe, which felt ridiculous, but you never know. \n\nBut no, I'm not really sad or down. Just annoyed with myself, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having trouble focusing on tasks or activities, even when you're not being distracted by something else?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. It's like my brain just glazes over even if it's completely quiet and nothing is going on. Like, I’ll think I’ve perfectly finished an EHR audit or checked a transfer note, and then my supervisor will pull me aside. Her name is Brenda, she's actually retiring next month and they're throwing this huge potluck for her in the breakroom. I signed up to bring my baked mac and cheese, which is a recipe I got from my aunt. She puts a little bit of dry mustard and nutmeg in it, which sounds weird, but it totally works. It really cuts the richness of the sharp cheddar. \n\nBut anyway, Brenda will pull up my chart and find that I missed a required field somewhere, or I put the wrong time on a medication follow-up. Or I'll have copied the completely wrong room number, even though I swear I looked right at it when I was typing. It's so embarrassing. Speaking of room numbers, they just renumbered our entire West Wing because of the new cardiac unit they built, and it is a complete maze now. The poor pharmacy delivery guys just wander around the halls looking so confused. I had to rescue one yesterday who was trying to find the staff elevator.\n\nDoctor: Did these problems with distraction and memory start after something particularly stressful or upsetting happened in your life?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, there wasn't one big thing that happened. It's just always been like this, honestly. Like, even back in elementary school, I remember my teachers always writing on my report cards that I needed to \"apply myself\" more. My third-grade teacher, Mr. Davies, he had this giant fish tank in the back of the classroom. It was amazing. He had these neon tetras and a plecostomus that just stuck to the glass all day. I used to stare at that tank instead of doing my math worksheets. I actually tried to set up a small aquarium in my apartment a few years ago, thinking it would be relaxing, you know? But keeping the water pH balanced is surprisingly complicated. I ended up giving the whole setup to my neighbor's kid. I mean, I don't feel like I have to get up and pace around or anything. I can sit through a whole movie just fine, usually. Unless it's one of those really long superhero movies. My brother dragged me to see the latest one, and I swear it was like three hours long. We got the giant tub of popcorn, the one where they give you free refills? But honestly, who gets up in the middle of a movie to get more popcorn? You'd miss the best parts. Plus, the floor in that theater was so sticky, it sounded like Velcro every time someone walked down the aisle. I kept thinking about how many germs must be on the bottom of my shoes. I actually keep a little bottle of hand sanitizer clipped to my badge reel at work, the kind that smells like cucumber melon. Sometimes I'll pick at my cuticles, but that's just a bad habit.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I'm impulsive, no. I'm actually pretty careful about most things. Like at work, obviously, you have to be. I double-check all my IV lines, even if it takes me forever because I keep losing my place on the MAR. But in my personal life... I don't know, I guess I'm not the type to just jump into things without thinking. Well, except for maybe when I'm grocery shopping. I always go in with a list, but then I see something random, like this weird brand of pickled asparagus I found last week. I didn't even know I liked pickled asparagus, but the jar had this really pretty vintage label on it. I'm a sucker for good packaging. My whole bathroom is full of these expensive lotions I bought just because the bottles looked nice on the shelf. They're all eucalyptus and mint scented, which is supposed to be good for stress relief. I read this article once that said keeping eucalyptus in your shower can clear your sinuses, so I actually bought a whole bundle of it from the farmer's market. But it dried out really fast and just made a huge mess all over the tile. I had to scrub the grout with an old toothbrush to get all the little crumbly bits out. \n\nBut as far as doing big, impulsive things without thinking? No, not really. I don't just, like, quit my job or buy a car on a whim or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble following instructions or paying attention in quiet environments, like when you're reading a book or working on a puzzle?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. Quiet environments are actually the hardest for me. If I try to read a book, I might get through half a page before I realize I haven't absorbed a single word because I started thinking about something else. Like, the other day I tried to read this mystery novel my sister lent me. She’s obsessed with true crime podcasts, too, the ones where they solve cold cases? She listens to them while she's gardening. She has this amazing tomato garden in her backyard. Last summer she had so many tomatoes she started canning her own salsa. I tried to help her one weekend, but the boiling water bath process is so tedious. You have to sterilize all the jars and make sure the seals are perfect, otherwise you can give yourself botulism, which is terrifying. We learned about that in nursing school, actually. The neurotoxin just completely paralyzes you. It’s wild how something so tiny can do that much damage. But anyway, reading... yeah. Puzzles are the same. I just can't stay focused on them if it's quiet. I end up just staring into space.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with distraction and memory are affecting your self-esteem?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really is. It’s... it’s actually getting pretty bad. I feel like I'm constantly messing up, especially at work. It’s gotten to the point where I’m actually on formal supervision right now. The other nurses have to double-check my charge-nurse follow-through, which is just... it's humiliating, honestly. I'm supposed to be a leader on the floor, and instead, I feel like I’m becoming a liability. And the other nurses are so nice about it, which almost makes it worse? Like, Sarah, the other charge nurse, she always brings in these amazing baked goods for the breakroom. Last week she made these lemon blueberry scones that were incredible. She said the secret is freezing the butter before you grate it into the flour. I tried making scones once, but they turned out like hockey pucks. I think I overworked the dough. Baking is so precise, you know? It’s not like cooking where you can just throw in a little bit of this and a little bit of that. My grandmother used to make the best chicken soup without ever measuring anything. She’d just taste the broth and know exactly what it needed. She had this huge cast-iron pot that she used for everything. I think my mom still has it... \n\nBut yeah, the supervision at work is really taking a toll on me. I just feel like I can't trust my own brain anymore, and it's making me feel terrible about myself.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble completing everyday tasks at home due to your difficulties with distraction and memory?\n\nPatient: Yeah, home is... it's a mess, honestly. He actually got me this physical planner, one of those really nice leather-bound ones? It's beautiful, the leather smells so good. It reminds me of this saddle shop near where I grew up. We used to go horseback riding every summer. My favorite horse was this sweet little chestnut mare named Buttercup. She was so gentle, but she had this funny habit of stopping to eat clover right in the middle of a trail ride. You'd have to pull her head up and click your tongue to get her moving again. I always brought her carrots, though. I read that horses love peppermints, too, but I was always too scared she'd accidentally bite my fingers. Their teeth are huge, you know? \n\nBut yeah, I forget to do basic things all the time at home. Like following up on an online order that never showed up. I just... it completely falls out of my head.\n\nDoctor: Are your difficulties with distraction and memory affecting your relationships with family and friends?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, honestly. I haven't noticed my friends being upset with me or anything. I mean, my husband gets frustrated sometimes, like I said, but my friends... we mostly just text or grab coffee. Actually, I met up with my friend Chloe for coffee last week at this new place that opened up near the hospital. They do all these fancy latte art things. She got a matcha latte and it had a perfect little swan drawn in the foam. I don't even know how they do that. I tried to froth milk at home once with one of those little hand-held wands, but it just splashed everywhere. It always ends up too sweet or too vinegary. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, relationships. Um, yeah, I don't think it's causing any major problems with my friends. At least, I haven't really noticed if it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant strain or impact on your relationships with your husband or others due to your difficulties with distraction and memory?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I don't really think it's causing a major strain. I mean, my husband definitely gets annoyed when I forget to do things around the house, like I mentioned before, but we don't really fight about it. It's more just him sighing loudly and doing it himself. He's actually really patient, usually. He works in IT, so I think he's just used to fixing things when they break or don't work right. It's just a little annoying for him sometimes, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had difficulty with distraction and memory, even as a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really has been a lifelong thing. My mom had to sit with me every single night at the kitchen table just to keep me on track. \n\nIt was this huge, heavy oak table my parents got at an estate sale. It had these really cool carved legs, like lion paws holding glass balls. I used to just stare at them while I was supposed to be doing my spelling words. We had this one squirrel that was missing half its tail, and he was so bold he would climb right up the screen door. My dad tried everything to keep him away. My sister put him in martial arts hoping it would burn off some of that energy, but now he just karate chops everything in the house. He actually broke one of her favorite vases last Thanksgiving. It was this beautiful blue and white porcelain one she got on her honeymoon in Greece. They went to Santorini, and the pictures were just stunning. All those white buildings with the bright blue roofs against the ocean. I've always wanted to go to Greece, but getting the time off work is so hard. Oh, ADHD. Yeah, no, I never got diagnosed or anything. Maybe? I mean, now that I'm actually thinking about it, I guess it makes sense. It's not like this is a new problem, you know? Even back in elementary school, my teachers were always writing on my report cards that I was bright but careless. I was constantly losing worksheets or just completely forgetting to turn in my homework, even if I actually did it. My mom had to sit with me at the kitchen table every single night just to keep me on track. That kitchen table was this big, heavy oak thing my grandpa built. He was a carpenter, and he made all this beautiful furniture for the family. He made this rocking chair for my parents when I was born, and it had these intricate carvings of vines and leaves on the armrests. I used to trace them with my fingers when I was little. My grandpa was so talented, but he was always covered in sawdust. He smelled like pine and peppermint, because he always kept those little red and white peppermints in his shirt pocket. He'd give me one whenever we visited, but I hated them. I'd just pretend to eat it and then spit it out in a napkin when he wasn't looking. I felt so bad, but they were just too spicy for me. I prefer fruity candy, like those strawberry candies with the soft center. They remind me of my grandma's house.

My third-grade teacher, Mr. Davies, he had this giant fish tank in the back of the classroom. It was amazing. He had these neon tetras and a plecostomus that just stuck to the glass all day. I used to stare at that tank instead of doing my math worksheets. I actually tried to set up a small aquarium in my apartment a few years ago, thinking it would be relaxing, you know? But keeping the water pH balanced is surprisingly complicated. I ended up giving the whole setup to my neighbor's kid. He’s super into marine biology now, which is pretty cool. \n\nBut yeah, no specific stressful event or anything. It’s just... always been how my brain works, as far back as I can remember.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I don't feel like I have to get up and pace around or anything. I can sit through a whole movie just fine, usually. Unless it's one of those really long superhero movies. My brother dragged me to see the latest one, and I swear it was like three hours long. We got the giant tub of popcorn, the one where they give you free refills? But honestly, who gets up in the middle of a movie to get more popcorn? You'd miss the best parts. Plus, the floor in that theater was so sticky, it sounded like Velcro every time someone walked down the aisle. I kept thinking about how many germs must be on the bottom of my shoes. I actually keep a little bottle of hand sanitizer clipped to my badge reel at work, the kind that smells like cucumber melon.
